### The Two Loading Mechanisms

Claude Code uses two distinct mechanisms for loading CLAUDE.md files:

#### Ancestor Loading (UP the directory tree)

When you start Claude Code, it walks **upward** from your current working directory toward the filesystem root and loads every CLAUDE.md it finds along the way. These files are loaded **immediately at startup**.

#### Descendant Loading (DOWN the directory tree)

CLAUDE.md files in subdirectories below your current working directory are **NOT loaded at launch**. They are only included when Claude reads files in those subdirectories during your session. This is known as **lazy loading**.

### Example Monorepo Structure

Consider a typical monorepo with separate directories for different components:

```
/mymonorepo/
├── CLAUDE.md          # Root-level instructions (shared across all components)
├── frontend/
│   └── CLAUDE.md      # Frontend-specific instructions
├── backend/
│   └── CLAUDE.md      # Backend-specific instructions
└── api/
    └── CLAUDE.md      # API-specific instructions
```

.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:2:name: deprecation-discipline
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:3:description: Code-as-liability sunset discipline. Sister to kiss-dry-yagni.md (prevents speculative ADD); this rule covers cleanup-phase REMOVAL + migration. Use when sunsetting features, replacing legacy systems, removing dead code that nobody owns but everybody depends on, OR planning lifecycle of new system at design time (deprecation planning starts at design time per Hyrum's Law).
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:4:paths: [".claude/rules/**", ".claude/agents/**", ".claude/commands/**", ".claude/skills/**/SKILL.md", "scripts/**", "tools/**", "docs/**"]
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:9:# Reference: constituents=[TIER-1-DIRECT @ Z:/claude-sota-installed/.claude/plugins/marketplaces/addy-agent-skills/skills/deprecation-and-migration/SKILL.md:10-12 @742dca5, TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION @ claude-sota-installed adaptation]; effective_tier=TIER-3-LOCAL-COMPOSITION per citation-discipline.md rule #8 [VERIFIED 2026-05-08 via Wave 82m-B sota-researcher Top-3 CITE-IMPORT #2 + Wave 82x codex T1 NEEDS-REVISION fix-forward F-001 cite-class reframe] — addyosmani/agent-skills (33,500 stars MIT, named-author Addy Osmani / Google Chrome team). Upstream verbatim at SKILL.md:10-12: "Code is a liability, not an asset. Every line of code has ongoing maintenance cost — bugs to fix, dependencies to update, security patches to apply, and new engineers to onboard. Deprecation is the discipline of removing code that no longer earns its keep, and migration is the process of moving users safely from the old to the new."
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:11:# Reference: TIER-2 sister rule `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/karpathy-adapted.md §3 Surgical Changes` (cite-import-AMBER per Section 14.5) — "Don't refactor things that aren't broken" interacts with deprecation: leave non-broken UNLESS deprecation-decision triggers (see §The deprecation decision below).
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:13:## The rule
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:17:## When this discipline applies
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:28:## Hyrum's Law (load-bearing)
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:32:This is why deprecation requires **active migration**, not just announcement. Users can't "just switch" when they depend on behaviors the replacement doesn't replicate.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:34:## The deprecation decision (5-question gate)
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:36:Before deprecating ANY surface — code, API, plugin, MCP, hook, rule, agent, skill — answer these in order:
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:40:3. **Does a replacement exist?** → If no, build the replacement FIRST. Never deprecate without an alternative. **Replacement readiness gate** (per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-003 / upstream SKILL.md:71-75): replacement must (a) cover critical use cases of the old system, (b) have documentation + migration guides, AND (c) be proven in production (not just "theoretically better") before deprecation starts.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:42:5. **What's the ongoing cost of NOT deprecating?** → Security risk + engineer time + opportunity cost of complexity.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:44:## Compulsory vs advisory deprecation
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:48:| **Advisory** | Replacement exists, soft-migrate path acceptable | Documentation deprecation note + warning at use site + parallel-deploy + telemetry |
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:49:| **Compulsory** | Breaking change required (security, broken contract, blocking next-gen feature) AND maintenance cost or risk justifies forcing migration | Hard removal at version cut OR `@deprecated` + compile error path + migration grace window |
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:51:**Default to advisory.** Use compulsory only when maintenance cost or risk justifies forcing migration. Compulsory deprecation requires migration tooling, documentation, and support; a deadline alone is not sufficient. Per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-002: upstream Osmani SKILL.md:62-65 mandates default-to-advisory + compulsory-with-tooling guardrail.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:53:## Migration patterns (4-stage)
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:55:1. **Parallel deploy**: ship new alongside old; both work; no behavior change
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:58:4. **Removal**: delete old code, dependencies, docs, references — verify ZERO active usage via metrics/logs/dependency analysis (NOT just `rg "<old-symbol>"` text search; per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-005 / upstream SKILL.md:112-118,201-206); then remove tests + docs + config + deprecation notices
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:60:### Churn Rule (load-bearing)
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:62:**If you own the surface being deprecated, you own user migration or backward-compatible updates; do not leave consumers to discover the path themselves.** Per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-004 / upstream SKILL.md:106. This makes the owner responsibility EXPLICIT — distinct from the "Announce-and-leave" anti-pattern below (which describes the failure mode this rule prevents).
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:64:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:66:- **Announce-and-leave**: emitting `@deprecated` without migration support — Hyrum's Law guarantees nobody migrates. Refuted by §Migration patterns 4-stage active discipline.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:67:- **Hard-remove-without-replacement**: deprecating before a viable migration path exists. Refuted by §The deprecation decision question 3.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:68:- **Maintain-forever**: keeping a legacy system because "users depend on it" without measuring the maintenance cost. Refuted by §The deprecation decision question 5.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:69:- **Speculative-deprecation**: marking code `@deprecated` because "we might replace it someday". This violates kiss-dry-yagni — only deprecate when a real replacement exists. Sister to "speculative ADD" anti-pattern.
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:72:## Sister-rule integration

.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:76:- `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/research-protocol.md` 3 gates — RECALL → INVESTIGATE → VERIFY before deprecating any surface (verify replacement exists + dependents migrated)
.claude/rules/deprecation-discipline.md:77:- `Z:/claude-sota/.claude/rules/codification-threshold.md` cycle-322 — symmetric: codification gate prevents premature ADD; deprecation gate prevents premature KEEP

.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:37:   - provenance: Read `docs/install-provenance.md` tail-rows for last-wave row
.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:41:5. **Continuity gate** — IF any Layer fails to load OR backend hash mismatches commit-body claim → emit `STALE-PRELOAD-NOTICE` in first user-facing turn per `cross-model-consensus.md §Env-funneled subagent stand-in disclosure mandate` Option 2 shape. .claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:53:## How to apply

.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:63:## Why
.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:65:Without SessionStart preload discipline, cross-session continuity decays via 4 failure modes:
.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:67:1. **Stale-belief propagation** (FM-20 row 8 W165 P1 catch) — orchestrator's belief about runtime state derives from commit-body claims that may be stale/fabricated. W164 F37 caught 14-fire silent-dual-write cascade where commit bodies claimed persist that never landed. Layer-1+Layer-2+Layer-3 preload + 5-backend hash verify catches at session-start NOT at next-fire apply (mia-pre-apply is apply-boundary; this rule is session-resume-boundary).
.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:69:2. **Karpathy §5 compounding-learning loss** — without Layer-2 MEMORY.md reload, orchestrator loses topic pointers to last-N waves' compiled wikis. Each new session re-derives context from CLAUDE.md cold-start instead of warm-starting from MEMORY.md pointers.
.claude/rules/sessionstart-preload-discipline.md:73:4. **Silent-dual-write fabrication** (W164 F37 + W166 F1 evidence ladder) — claims of dual-write persist that never landed accumulate without session-resume verification gate. Sister rule fm20-path-drift-cascade.md catches via row 9 asymmetric-dual-write sub-class.

.claude/rules/sota-research-architecture.md:7:# SOTA Research Architecture (SRA) — multi-dimensional convergence gate
.claude/rules/sota-research-architecture.md:9:# Reference: TIER-1-DIRECT user directive 2026-05-08 verbatim "improve your research architecture, you need to make sure they have the ability to gather muti dimensional informaiton, github and beyond, to find the convergence, it is the very foundation of our entire architecutre" — codifies the methodology improvement mandate triggered by Wave 102 audit's over-applied REJECT verdicts (Ship 2T trufflehog AGPL-3.0 over-applied to CLI-binary-use; KuzuDB recommended at 7-month-stale despite "SOTA" framing).

.claude/rules/sota-research-architecture.md:15:## The rule
.claude/rules/sota-research-architecture.md:17:A repo qualifies as **SOTA** for eee runtime adoption if and only if it satisfies **multi-dimensional convergence** across 10 independent dimensions, with **use-class precision** per dimension. Flat criteria ("AGPL = REJECT" / "stars >500") produce false-positive rejects + miss real risk. Convergence gate = ≥7 of 10 dimensions PASS for use-class, with no critical dimension (D1 license-use-class blocking, D6 use-class incompatible) FAILED.
.claude/rules/sota-research-architecture.md:19:**Replacement viability**: any proposed replacement MUST satisfy D1-D10 AND be ≤ incumbent's last-push age (D2 freshness gate). A staler replacement is NOT a valid SOTA recommendation.

.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:14:## The rule
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:16:Every launch should be **reversible, observable, and incremental**. The goal is not just to deploy — it's to deploy safely with monitoring in place, a rollback plan ready, and a clear definition of success.

.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:28:7. Any deployment that carries risk (all of them per Osmani)

.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:135:## Deploy lifecycle integration (D-namespace; adjacent to T-namespace, not collision)
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:137:**Critical namespace note**: per Wave 82x codex T1 fix-forward F-002, the T-namespace (T1-T7) is FULLY OWNED by `cross-model-consensus.md` (T1=pre-edit / T2=working-tree / T3=post-commit / T4=post-push / T5=plan-stage / T6=stop-gate / T7=ask-without-act). This rule introduces a SEPARATE **D-namespace** for deploy-phase gates that fire AFTER the T-cycle completes:
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:141:| T1-T7 | edit / commit / push / plan / stop / ask-without-act lifecycle | codex T-hooks per `cross-model-consensus.md` | cross-model-consensus rule |

.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:145:D1 + D2 are deploy-phase extensions ADJACENT TO, not replacements for, the cross-model T1-T7 lifecycle. The full edit→ship→watch chain is `T1→T2→T3→T4→T5→T6→T7→[git push remote / deploy boundary]→D1→D2`.
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:147:## Anti-patterns
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:149:- **Ship-and-pray**: deploying without monitoring in place. Refuted by §Observable invariant 2.
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:150:- **Big-bang launch**: 0% → 100% traffic flip without staging. Refuted by §Incremental invariant 3.
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:151:- **Irreversible launch with no override audit**: shipping a one-way door without explicit HIGH-RISK classification. Refuted by §Reversible invariant 1.
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:152:- **Skip-monitoring-window**: declaring launch "done" at deploy-time without 24-72h observation. Refuted by §Post-launch monitoring window.
.claude/rules/launch-discipline.md:153:- **Confuse T5 plan-review with T6 pre-launch**: plan-time and deploy-time are distinct gates. Plan-review checks design soundness; pre-launch checks deployment readiness. Run both, not one.

.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:32:## The rule (two empirical Forward Disciplines for codex T1 Path P prompt construction)
.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:34:Codex T1 Path P prompts must be SCOPE-CONTROLLED to prevent Pattern B HONEST-NON-FINDING (HNF) verdicts from budget exhaustion. Two empirical sub-rules cover two distinct fire classes.
.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:36:## Forward Discipline #1 — Repo-scope threshold
.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:40:- `sub_package_count > 5` — **where `sub_package_count` means independently reviewable package/module surfaces** (e.g., distinct `pyproject.toml` / `package.json` / `Cargo.toml` roots in a monorepo), NOT merely top-level directories or platform-integration sub-folders
.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:45:- Identify the SINGLE highest-value primitive (e.g., "Pregel BSP" for langgraph; "Handoff primitive" for openai-agents-python)
.claude/rules/codex-t1-pattern-b-forward-discipline.md:46:- Build codex T1 prompt that explicitly says: "audit the X primitive ONLY; do NOT explore other sub-packages OR multi-platform integrations"

.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:26:## The rule
.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:28:When working in long-arc /loop sessions (≥4h wall-clock OR multi-Bundle ship cycles OR cron-driven autonomous arcs), context bloat dominates token cost. The local runtime treats late blind autocompact as a failure mode: CCBP documents the upstream default as roughly 95 unless overridden (`Z:/repos/deps/claude-code-best-practice-shan/best-practice/claude-settings.md:826 @ HEAD 48f2cebeb88b389b27231c418ceadb65baf813fd`), while this repo may set a lower local target in `.claude/settings.json`. Blind autocompact produces lossy summaries (per `karpathy-adapted.md §5`). SOTA auto-compact discipline is **pre-emptive, ranked, and routes-around the autocompact mechanism entirely**.
.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:30:## Ranked recipe (codex T1 ranking from /goal P2[B] partial trace 2026-05-13)
.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:32:### Rank #1 — context-mode `ctx_batch_execute` (PRIMARY, ~98% savings)
.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:52:### Rank #2 — repomix pack→grep workflow (~70% compression for repo-scope audits)
.claude/rules/auto-compact-discipline.md:59:3. **Skill persist** (if learning is durably reusable): **DO NOT** use `mcp__repomix__generate_skill(directory, skillName)` for routine auto-compact reuse — that primitive writes `.claude/skills/<name>/SKILL.md` outside the documented install/provenance path, violating cardinal-rule-5 install-priority + cardinal-rule-6 official-native-channel. Instead: (a) persist topic-scoped findings to `.claude/projects/*/memory/` per Karpathy §5 Layer-3 compiled wiki (no install-class artifact created), OR (b) escalate as install-class candidate via sota-researcher dispatch with convergence-gate Axis-1+2+3 verification + Section 14 marketplace install path + `docs/install-provenance.md` row. Local `generate_skill` invocations bypass T1+T2 audit and provenance log.

.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:17:## The rule
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:19:Sota-researcher / architect / general agent dispatches that surface SOTA candidates MUST include a **harness-fit verification step** asking "is this applicable to OUR specific architecture?" — beyond the convergence-gate Axis 1+2+3 (T1 sources / T2 practitioners / Axis 3 stability) that scores SOTA-status. Without harness-fit, candidates pass convergence-gate AND still fail at ship-target verification because of architectural mismatch invisible to upstream-source-only audit.
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:21:This is **convergence-gate Axis 4** (harness-fit) — separate from Axis 1+2+3 (which classify SOTA-status of the candidate ITSELF) and applied AFTER convergence-gate PASS, BEFORE ship-target verification.
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:23:## When this rule applies
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:25:Whenever an agent (sota-researcher / architect / general-purpose / Explore / code-reviewer / debugger) returns a ship-candidate ranking from a Wave-N dispatch:
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:27:1. The agent's convergence-gate verdict is necessary BUT NOT SUFFICIENT for ship-readiness
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:28:2. The candidate may be valid SOTA AND inapplicable to claude-sota
.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:29:3. Apply the 4-axis harness-fit probe BEFORE writing T1 consult prompt OR queuing the ship

.claude/rules/agent-harness-fit-verification.md:71:- **Adopting any installer marked HARD-GATE / `disable-model-invocation: true` / interactive setup gate** — refuted by iter-84 brainstorming + iter-92 mattpocock cohort (n=2 same-pattern). CR-7 Phase 2/3 transition predicates do NOT relax this — the install-time HARD-GATE fires BEFORE permission mode applies. **Reject the current install shape, NOT the project forever** — if upstream removes interactive gates and ships canonical `.claude-plugin/marketplace.json`, fresh Probe 4/5/6 + SRA D6/T1 re-verification can promote install-class disposition.
